# Golden C++ side of the Teralynx parity harness, part oracle (the 27 oracle scripts) (crates/sai-teralynx-parity, README "Parity", kind golden).
# the oracle runner sai_script.cpp on the C++ libsai, per oracle script (after c/oracle_prep.cpp drops the frame-sender steps): how it ended, the sha256 of the script (the INPUT) and what oracle-prep dropped, per step the IFCS lines (the mock's with the @B lines) as count, sha256 and head, and the runner's stdout without times (stored whole: the answers).
# The C++ libsai of ai_re/nix/packages/sai-teralynx (the CS8210's libsai, built as its Makefile does) and the
# harness's programs (unmodified test_ifcs, frontend_driver.cpp, sai_script.cpp; build-fixtures.sh) ran on the SAME
# mock libifcs; the harness (sai-teralynx-parity --capture) rendered the C++ side's output below, normalised.

# Command (the fixtures: rust/sai/crates/sai-teralynx-parity/build-fixtures.sh FX <tests+frontend files> <ifcs-api-headers>/include
# <sairedis SAI/inc> <C++ libsai> <nlohmann_json>/include, MOCK_LIBIFCS = the mock built from rust/sai/tests/teralynx-mock):
#   sai-teralynx-parity --fixtures FX --work /tmp/claude-1001/p2 --cpp-lib <C++ libsai> --areas crates/sai-backend-teralynx/tests/areas.txt \
#     --expected crates/sai-backend-teralynx/tests/test_ifcs-expected.txt --allow crates/sai-teralynx-parity/tests/parity-allow.txt \
#     --pending crates/sai-teralynx-parity/tests/parity-pending.txt --capture <dir>      (then this header over the records)
# Seeds: none (nothing is random); the capture runs the C++ side twice and requires equal records.
# Normalised: the work directory is <work>, the fixtures directory <fixtures>, nix store paths <store>; oracle times are ms=*.
# Records (tab-separated; '|' lines are the head of the record above):
#   script NAME STATUS INPUT-SHA256 STEPS SKIPPED   one per script (SKIPPED: oracle-prep's last line)
#   step LINES SHA256 TAG                           one per step ('init' first, then 'M step <tag>'), + its head
#   O LINE / E LINE                                 the runner's stdout (STEP lines without times) / its sorted 'event' lines
# A digest is the sha256 of the unit's lines, each followed by a newline; it decides. The head is the unit's first 4 lines,
# each cut to 160 characters, so a difference can be named; a difference beyond the head is reported by its counts and digest.
# NOT to be regenerated from the Rust output: the records are the C++ side's, and only the C++ side may write them
# (sai-teralynx-parity --capture, run on the C++ libsai). A deliberate change of behaviour of the Rust libsai edits the
# affected records by hand, or adds an entry to tests/parity-allow.txt, with a reason.
# Live == golden at capture: sai-teralynx-parity --fixtures FX --work <w> --cpp-lib <C++ libsai> <lists> --check-golden crates/sai-teralynx-parity/tests/golden: GREEN, the live C++ renders to these records byte for byte (a unit test of the crate, tests/golden.rs, shows a changed byte fails).
